=== Vancomycin (Merck Manual Professional) ===
Source: https://www.merckmanuals.com/professional/drug-names-generic-and-brand (productid 638)
Retrieved: 2026-09-25
NURSING QUICK-REFERENCE SUMMARY
Auto-generated on 2026-09-25 from the prescribing information in this file; full details follow below.

Drug Class: Vancomycin is an intravenous and oral glycopeptide antibiotic.

Routes: injection (IV/IM/SC), oral; Common adult doses: 2 g/day IV/PO per FDA-approved labeling; individualize IV dosage to patient age, weight, indication for use, and serum drug concentration monitoring.

Common Side Effects (excluding routine GI upset): dizziness, hearing loss, ototoxicity, tinnitus, vertigo, azotemia, interstitial nephritis, renal failure (unspecified), agranulocytosis, anemia, eosinophilia, hemolytic anemia, neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, chills, fatigue, fever, peripheral edema, c. difficile-associated diarrhea, infection, pseudomembranous colitis, superinfection, depression, headache, insomnia

In an Overdose: No overdose/antidote information is listed in this file.

Key Nursing Considerations: Contraindications/precautions: Vancomycin is contraindicated in patients who have a history of vancomycin hypersensitivity. Vancomycin solutions containing dextrose may be contraindicated in patients with history of corn hypersensitivity or corn product hypersensitivity. Administer orally or via a nasogastric tube. Oral Administration Administration Details Intravenous Administration Description Reconstitution/Preparation Lyophilized powder single-dose vials for injection Reconstitute vials with Sterile...

Indication Specific Dosing
  Indications
    Related Indications
      Heading
      for the treatment of nonspecific LRTIs
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dose is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dose is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dose is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The FDA-approved dose is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading
          Infants and Children 3 months to 11 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 80 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg IV in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dose is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Infants and Children 3 months to 11 years
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 60 to 80 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The FDA-approved dose is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading
          Infants 1 to 2 months
          Dosage Description
          45 to 60 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter.  Consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg IV in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dose is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr less than 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The FDA-approved dose is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested.
          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr 0.7 to 0.9 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 24 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The FDA-approved dose is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested.
          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr 1 to 1.2 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 24 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The FDA-approved dose is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested.
          Heading
          Neonates 28 weeks gestation and younger and SCr less than 0.5 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The FDA-approved dose is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested.
          Heading
          Neonates 28 weeks gestation and younger and SCr 0.5 to 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 24 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The FDA-approved dose is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested.
          Heading
          Neonates 28 weeks gestation and younger and SCr 0.8 to 1 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 24 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The FDA-approved dose is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested.
        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage†
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by continuous IV infusion of 30 to 60 mg/kg/day IV to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (age 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years.
          Heading
          Neonates
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations.
      Heading
      for the treatment of community-acquired pneumonia (CAP)

      Heading
      for the treatment of hospital-acquired or postprocedural pleural empyema
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat for at least 2 weeks after drainage and defervescence as part of combination therapy.
          Heading

          Dosage Description

Treat for at least 2 weeks after drainage and defervescence as part of combination therapy.

          Infants 1 to 2 months
          Dosage Description
          45 to 60 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter.   Consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg IV in critically ill patients. Treat for at least 2 weeks after drainage and defervescence as part of combination therapy.
        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage†
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L. Treat for a minimum of 2 weeks after drainage and defervescence as part of combination therapy.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mg/L. Treat for at least 2 weeks after drainage and defervescence as part of combination therapy. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (age 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years.
      Heading
      for the treatment of lung abscess
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total treatment duration of at least 3 to 4 weeks based on clinical response.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total treatment duration of at least 3 to 4 weeks based on clinical response.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. Treat until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total treatment duration of at least 3 to 4 weeks based on clinical response.

    Routes
    Heading
    For the treatment of serious gram-positive infections, including lower respiratory tract infections (LRTIs) such as community-acquired pneumonia (CAP), nosocomial pneumonia, lung abscess, and pleural empyema
    Related Indications
      Heading
      for the treatment of native valve endocarditis due to viridans group streptococci and nonenterococcal group D streptococci
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for 4 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          30 to 60 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day) for 4 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider adding gentamicin for relatively resistant strains. Consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dosage is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr less than 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for 4 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider adding gentamicin for relatively resistant strains. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. The FDA-approved dosage is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours.
          Heading

Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day) for 4 to 6 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day) for at least 4 to 6 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. Consider gentamicin for the first 3 to 5 days. The FDA-approved dosage is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.

          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr less than 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for at least 4 to 6 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider gentamicin for the first 3 to 5 days. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. The FDA-approved dosage is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours.

        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage†
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L for 4 to 6 weeks.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by continuous IV infusion of 30 to 60 mg/kg/day IV to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL for at least 4 to 6 weeks; consider gentamicin for the first 3 to 5 days. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (aged 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years.
          Heading
          Neonates
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL for at least 4 to 6 weeks; consider gentamicin for the first 3 to 5 days. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations.
      Heading

          60 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours for 6 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg IV in critically ill patients. Consult with an infectious diseases specialist; combination therapy is dependent on most likely pathogens. The FDA-approved dosage is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading

          Dosage Description

The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. Consult with an infectious diseases specialist; combination therapy is dependent on most likely pathogens. The FDA-approved dosage is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours.

        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage†
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L. Consult with an infectious diseases specialist; combination therapy and duration are dependent on most likely pathogens.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by continuous IV infusion of 30 to 60 mg/kg/day IV to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL for 6 weeks. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (aged 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years. Consult with an infectious diseases specialist; combination therapy is dependent on most likely pathogens.
          Heading
          Neonates
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL for 6 weeks. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations. Consult with an infectious diseases specialist; combination therapy is dependent on most likely pathogens.

      for the treatment of initial episode of pseudomembranous colitis due to C. difficile infection and first recurrence in patients previously treated with metronidazole

      Heading
      for the treatment of fulminant pseudomembranous colitis due to C. difficile infection
      Routes
        Heading
        Oral dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          500 mg PO or via nasogastric tube 4 times daily with IV metronidazole. If clinical improvement after 48 to 72 hours, consider decreasing the dose to 125 mg PO every 6 hours for 10 days. If an ileus is present, consider adding vancomycin as a retention enema.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 500 mg/dose) PO or via nasogastric tube 4 times daily with IV metronidazole for 10 days. If an ileus is present, consider adding vancomycin as a retention enema.
        Heading
        Rectal dosage†
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          500 mg in 100 mL of 0.9% Sodium Chloride Injection rectally every 6 hours as adjunctive therapy if ileus present. Other dosing regimens have been described in case reports including 500 mg every 4 hours and 1 g every 8 to 12 hours. Volumes of 250 to 500 mL of 0.9% Sodium Chloride Injection have also been described.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          500 mg in 100 mL of 0.9% Sodium Chloride Injection rectally every 8 hours as needed until improvement as adjunctive therapy if ileus present.
      Heading

          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO 4 times daily for 10 days.

          Heading
          Adults

          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO 4 times daily for 7 days, then 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO 3 times daily for 7 days, then 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO twice daily for 7 days, then 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO once daily for 7 days, then 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO every other day for 7 days, then 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO every 3 days for 7 days; OR 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO 4 times daily for 14 days, then 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO twice daily for 7 to 14 days, then 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO once daily for 7 to 14 days, then 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO every 2 to 3 days for 2 to 8 weeks; OR 10 mg/kg/dose (Max: 125 mg/dose) PO 4 times daily for 14 days, followed by rifaximin for 14 days.

    Heading
    For the treatment of enterocolitis due to S. aureus
    Routes
      Heading
      Intravenous dosage
      Route Age Groups
        Heading
        Adults
        Dosage Description
        15 mg/kg/dose IV as a single dose within 120 minutes prior to the surgical incision. No intraoperative redosing and a duration of prophylaxis less than 24 hours for most procedures are recommended. A longer prophylaxis duration of 48 hours for certain cardiothoracic procedures is controversial. Vancomycin is recommended as alternate therapy for patients with beta-lactam allergy undergoing cardiothoracic, hernia repair, neurosurgical, orthopedic, clean urologic, vascular, heart or lung transplantation, and plastic surgery procedures. Vancomycin is also recommended in combination with another appropriate antimicrobial (i.e., aminoglycoside, aztreonam, or fluoroquinolone) as alternate therapy for patients with beta-lactam allergy undergoing gastroduodenal, biliary tract, hysterectomy, urologic with prosthesis, and abdominal transplantation procedures.
        Heading
        Infants, Children, and Adolescents
        Dosage Description
        15 mg/kg/dose IV as a single dose within 120 minutes prior to the surgical incision. No intraoperative redosing and a duration of prophylaxis less than 24 hours for most procedures are recommended. A longer prophylaxis duration of 48 hours for certain cardiothoracic procedures is controversial. Vancomycin is recommended as alternate therapy for patients with beta-lactam allergy undergoing cardiothoracic, hernia repair, neurosurgical, orthopedic, clean urologic, vascular, heart or lung transplantation, and plastic surgery procedures. Vancomycin is also recommended in combination with another appropriate antimicrobial (i.e., aminoglycoside, aztreonam, or fluoroquinolone) as alternate therapy for patients with beta-lactam allergy undergoing gastroduodenal, biliary tract, urologic with prosthesis, and abdominal transplantation procedures.
    Heading
    For surgical infection prophylaxis†
    Related Indications
      Heading
      for the treatment of meningitis† and ventriculitis†
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat for 10 to 14 days. In patients with repeatedly positive CSF cultures on appropriate therapy, treat for 10 to 14 days after the last positive culture. Add rifampin if MRSA is susceptible and prosthetic material is present. For pneumococcal meningitis, consider adding rifampin if ceftriaxone/cefotaxime MIC is more than 2 mcg/mL.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat for 10 to 14 days. In patients with repeatedly positive CSF cultures on appropriate therapy, treat for 10 to 14 days after the last positive culture. Add rifampin if MRSA is susceptible and prosthetic material is present. For pneumococcal meningitis, consider adding rifampin if ceftriaxone/cefotaxime MIC is more than 2 mcg/mL.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, a loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV may be considered in critically ill patients. Treat for 10 to 14 days. In patients with repeatedly positive CSF cultures on appropriate therapy, treat for 10 to 14 days after the last positive culture. Add rifampin if MRSA is susceptible and prosthetic material is present. For pneumococcal meningitis, consider adding rifampin if ceftriaxone/cefotaxime MIC is more than 2 mcg/mL.
          Heading
          Obese Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Treat for 10 to 14 days. In patients with repeatedly positive CSF cultures on appropriate therapy, treat for 10 to 14 days after the last positive culture. Add rifampin if MRSA is susceptible and prosthetic material is present. For pneumococcal meningitis, consider adding rifampin if ceftriaxone/cefotaxime MIC is more than 2 mcg/mL.

        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L. Treat for 10 to 14 days. In patients with repeatedly positive CSF cultures on appropriate therapy, treat for 10 to 14 days after the last positive culture. Add rifampin if MRSA is susceptible and prosthetic material is present. For pneumococcal meningitis, consider adding rifampin if ceftriaxone/cefotaxime MIC is more than 2 mcg/mL.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose (20 mg/kg/dose in critically ill patients) IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (aged 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years. Treat for 10 to 14 days. In patients with repeatedly positive CSF cultures on appropriate therapy, treat for 10 to 14 days after the last positive culture. Add rifampin if MRSA is susceptible and prosthetic material is present. For pneumococcal meningitis, consider adding rifampin if ceftriaxone/cefotaxime MIC is more than 2 mcg/mL.
          Heading
          Neonates
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations. Treat for 10 to 14 days. In patients with repeatedly positive CSF cultures on appropriate therapy, treat for 10 to 14 days after the last positive culture. Add rifampin if MRSA is susceptible and prosthetic material is present. For pneumococcal meningitis, consider adding rifampin if ceftriaxone/cefotaxime MIC is more than 2 mcg/mL.
        Heading
        Intrathecal dosage (preservative-free formulations only)
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          5 to 20 mg/day intrathecally; however, most studies have used a 10 or 20 mg dose. Use in addition to systemic vancomycin therapy. Adjust dose as necessary based on vancomycin CSF concentrations and MIC of the organism.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          5 to 20 mg/day intrathecally; however, most studies have used a 10 or 20 mg dose. Use in addition to systemic vancomycin therapy. Adjust dose as necessary based on vancomycin CSF concentrations and MIC of the organism.
        Heading
        Intraventricular dosage (preservative-free formulations only)
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults with slit ventricles with external ventricular drain output of less than 50 mL/24 hours
          Dosage Description
          5 mg intraventricularly every third day. Use in addition to systemic vancomycin therapy. Adjust dose as necessary based on vancomycin CSF concentrations and MIC of the organism.
          Heading
          Adults with slit ventricles with external ventricular drain output of 50 to 100 mL/24 hours
          Dosage Description
          5 mg intraventricularly every second day. Use in addition to systemic vancomycin therapy. Adjust dose as necessary based on vancomycin CSF concentrations and MIC of the organism.
          Heading
          Adults with slit ventricles with external ventricular drain output of 100 to 150 mL/24 hours
          Dosage Description
          5 mg intraventricularly once daily. Use in addition to systemic vancomycin therapy. Adjust dose as necessary based on vancomycin CSF concentrations and MIC of the organism.

          Dosage Description

          Heading

          Dosage Description
          15 mg intraventricularly once daily. Use in addition to systemic vancomycin therapy. Adjust dose as necessary based on vancomycin CSF concentrations and MIC of the organism.

      Heading

          Obese Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Treat for 3 to 6 weeks after surgical drainage; therapy may be extended if osteomyelitis is present.
          Heading
          Infants and Children 3 months to 11 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 80 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg IV in critically ill patients. Treat for 3 to 6 weeks after surgical drainage; therapy may be extended if osteomyelitis is present.

        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L. Treat for 3 to 6 weeks after surgical drainage; therapy may be extended if osteomyelitis is present.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose (20 mg/kg/dose in critically ill patients) IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (aged 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years. Treat for 3 to 6 weeks after surgical drainage; therapy may be extended if osteomyelitis is present.
          Heading
          Neonates
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations. Treat for 3 to 6 weeks after surgical drainage; therapy may be extended if osteomyelitis is present.

    Routes
    Heading
    For the treatment of CNS infections†, including meningitis†, ventriculitis† (including shunt-related infections), brain abscess†, subdural empyema†, epidural abscess†, and septic thrombosis of the cavernous or dural venous sinus†
    Related Indications
      Heading
      for rapid vancomycin desensitization† (Lerner and Dwyer protocol)
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          Administer 5 infusions in succession at an initial rate of 30 mL/hour and increase by 30 mL/hour as tolerated every 5 minutes (Max: 300 mL/hour). Infusion 1: 0.02 mg/100 mL IV; Infusion 2: 0.2 mg/100 mL IV; Infusion 3: 2 mg/100 mL IV; Infusion 4: 20 mg/100mL IV; Infusion 5: 500 mg/250 mL IV. Immediately administer the required vancomycin dose after Infusion 5. If the patient does not tolerate an infusion, stop the infusion and reinfuse at the previously tolerated infusion at the highest tolerated rate; may repeat this step up to 3 times for any given concentration.
      Heading
      for slow vancomycin desensitization† (Lin protocol)
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          Administer 1 infusion on 6 consecutive days at a rate of 100 mL/hour. Infusion 1: 0.5 mg/500 mL IV; Infusion 2: 5 mg/500 mL IV; Infusion 3: 50 mg/500 mL IV; Infusion 4: 50 mg/500 mL IV; Infusion 5: 250 mg/500 mL IV; Infusion 6: 500 mg/500 mL IV. Administer the required vancomycin dose on day 7.
    Routes
    Heading
    For vancomycin desensitization† in patients with vancomycin hypersensitivity who require vancomycin therapy
    Routes
      Heading
      Intravenous dosage
      Route Age Groups
        Heading
        Adults
        Dosage Description
        20 mg/kg/dose (Max: 2 g/dose) IV every 8 hours starting at the time of labor or rupture of membranes and continuing until delivery. Antibiotics administered for at least 4 hours before delivery have been found to be highly effective at preventing the transmission of Group B Streptococcus.
        Heading
        Adolescents
        Dosage Description
        20 mg/kg/dose (Max: 2 g/dose) IV every 8 hours starting at the time of labor or rupture of membranes and continuing until delivery. Antibiotics administered for at least 4 hours before delivery have been found to be highly effective at preventing the transmission of Group B Streptococcus.
    Heading
    For perinatal Group B streptococcal infection prophylaxis† in individuals allergic to penicillin
    Routes
      Heading
      Intravenous dosage
      Route Age Groups
        Heading
        Adults
        Dosage Description
        20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Guidelines recommend vancomycin in patients at increased risk for MRSA or enterococcal infections.
        Heading
        Obese Adults
        Dosage Description
        20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter.     Heading
    For the empiric treatment of febrile neutropenia†
    Related Indications
      Heading
      for the treatment of bacteremia
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia. The FDA-approved dosage is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years

          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr less than 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia. The FDA-approved dosage is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours.
          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr 0.7 to 0.9 mg/dL

The FDA-approved dosage is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours.
          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr 1 to 1.2 mg/dL

          Heading
          Neonates 28 weeks gestation and younger and SCr less than 0.5 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia. The FDA-approved dosage is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours.
          Heading
          Neonates 28 weeks gestation and younger and SCr 0.5 to 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 24 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia. The FDA-approved dosage is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours.
          Heading
          Neonates 28 weeks gestation and younger and SCr 0.8 to 1 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 24 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. The FDA-approved dosage is 15 mg/kg/dose IV initially, then 10 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours for the first week of life, then increase to every 8 hours.
        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage†
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (age 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia.
          Heading
          Neonates
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations. MRSA guidelines recommend a treatment duration of at least 2 weeks for uncomplicated bacteremia and 4 to 6 weeks for complicated bacteremia.
      Heading
      for the treatment of sepsis
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Start within 1 hour for sepsis or within 3 hours for possible sepsis without shock. Duration of therapy should be individualized based on individual and infection-specific factors. Assess patient daily for de-escalation of antimicrobial therapy based on pathogen identification and/or adequate clinical response. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Start within 1 hour for sepsis or within 3 hours for possible sepsis without shock. Duration of therapy should be individualized based on individual and infection-specific factors. Assess patient daily for de-escalation of antimicrobial therapy based on pathogen identification and/or adequate clinical response. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV. Start within 1 hour for septic shock or within 3 hours for probable sepsis without shock. Duration of therapy should be individualized based on individual and infection-specific factors. Assess patient daily for de-escalation of antimicrobial therapy based on pathogen identification and/or adequate clinical response. The FDA-approved dosage is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description

    Routes
    Heading
    For the treatment of bacteremia and sepsis
    Related Indications
      Heading
      for the treatment of unspecified osteomyelitis due to methicillin-resistant S. aureus
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours for at least 8 weeks, which may be followed by long-term suppressive therapy; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. May consider the addition of rifampin; for patients with concurrent bacteremia, add rifampin after bacteremia clearance. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day) for at least 8 weeks, which may be followed by long-term suppressive therapy; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. May consider the addition of rifampin; for patients with concurrent bacteremia, add rifampin after bacteremia clearance. The FDA-approved dosage is 500 mg IV every 6 hours or 1 g IV every 12 hours.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. Treat for 2 to 4 days or until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total duration of 3 to 4 weeks for uncomplicated cases. A longer course (i.e., 4 to 6 weeks or longer) may be needed for severe or complicated infections. The FDA-approved dosage is 40 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours.
          Heading

      Heading
      for the treatment of infectious arthritis† due to methicillin-resistant S. aureus
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat for 1 to 2 weeks or until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for 2 to 4 weeks.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat for 1 to 2 weeks or until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for 2 to 4 weeks.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. Treat for 2 to 4 days or until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total duration of 10 to 14 days for uncomplicated cases. A longer course (i.e., 3 to 4 weeks) may be needed for children with slower clinical response, inadequate source control, more resistant pathogens, or persistently elevated CRP.

          45 to 60 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg IV in critically ill patients. A longer course (i.e., 3 to 4 weeks) may be needed for children with slower clinical response, inadequate source control, more resistant pathogens, or persistently elevated CRP.
          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr less than 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. Treat for 14 to 21 days or until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total duration of 4 to 6 weeks. A longer course (several months) may be needed for severe or complicated infections.

        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L. Treat for 1 to 2 weeks or until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for 2 to 4 weeks.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by continuous IV infusion of 30 to 60 mg/kg/day IV to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Treat for 2 to 4 days or until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total duration of 10 to 14 days for uncomplicated cases. A longer course (i.e., 3 to 4 weeks) may be needed for children with slower clinical response, inadequate source control, more resistant pathogens, or persistently elevated CRP. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (age 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years.
          Heading
          Neonates
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Treat for 14 to 21 days or until clinically improved, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total duration of 4 to 6 weeks. A longer course (several months) may be needed for severe or complicated infections. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations.

Consider loading dose in critically ill patients.

      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours for 2 to 3 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Generally, 2 weeks is appropriate for most patients; severe cases or individuals that are immunocompromised may require a longer duration.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description

          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day) for 2 to 3 weeks; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. Generally, 2 weeks is appropriate for most patients; severe cases or individuals that are immunocompromised may require a longer duration.
          Heading

          Heading

      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose (Max: 2,000 mg/dose) IV every 8 to 12 hours for 7 to 14 days for moderate or severe infections in people with risk factors for methicillin resistant S. aureus; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter.           Dosage Description
          60 to 80 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day) for 14 to 21 days plus ampicillin; sulbactam, piperacillin; tazobactam, or a carbapenem for patients with underlying conditions; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter.       Heading
      for the treatment of complicated community-acquired, healthcare-acquired, or hospital-acquired intraabdominal infections† with adequate source control
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat for 3 to 7 days as part of combination therapy. Complicated infections include peritonitis and appendicitis complicated by rupture, and intraabdominal abscess.

        Heading
        Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L. Treat for 3 to 7 days as part of combination therapy. Complicated infections include peritonitis and appendicitis complicated by rupture, and intraabdominal abscess.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose (20 mg/kg/dose in critically ill patients) IV loading dose, followed by continuous IV infusion of 30 to 60 mg/kg/day IV to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (aged 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years. Treat for 3 to 7 days as part of combination therapy. Complicated infections include peritonitis and appendicitis complicated by rupture, and intraabdominal abscess.
          Heading
          Neonates
          Dosage Description
          10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations. Treat for 7 to 10 days as part of combination therapy. Vancomycin is an option for necrotizing enterocolitis.
      Heading
      for the treatment of peritoneal dialysis-related peritonitis†
      Routes
        Heading
        Intermittent Intraperitoneal dosage†
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 30 mg/kg/dose intraperitoneally every 5 to 7 days; adjust dose based on serum concentration target of more than 15 mcg/mL. Supplemental doses may be needed for automated PD. Treat for 14 to 21 days.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          30 mg/kg/dose intraperitoneally once, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose every 3 to 5 days; adjust dose based on serum concentration and redose when less than 15 mcg/mL. Treat for 14 to 21 days.
        Heading
        Continuous Intraperitoneal dosage†
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          30 mg/kg/bag intraperitoneal loading dose, followed by 1.5 mg/kg/bag in each dialysate exchange. Treat for 14 to 21 days.
          Heading
          Infants, Children, and Adolescents
          Dosage Description
          1,000 mg/L intraperitoneal loading dose, followed by 25 mg/L in each dialysate exchange. Treat for 14 to 21 days.

          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Antibiotics should be discontinued within 24 hours. Uncomplicated infections include acute appendicitis without perforation, abscess, or local peritonitis; traumatic bowel perforations repaired within 12 hours; acute cholecystitis without perforation; and ischemic, non-perforated bowel.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day). Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Antibiotics should be discontinued within 24 hours. Uncomplicated infections include acute appendicitis without perforation, abscess, or local peritonitis; traumatic bowel perforations repaired within 12 hours; acute cholecystitis without perforation; and ischemic, non-perforated bowel.
          Heading
          Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
          Dosage Description
          60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day). Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in non-obese pediatric patients. However, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. Antibiotics should be discontinued within 24 hours. Uncomplicated infections include acute appendicitis without perforation, abscess, or local peritonitis; traumatic bowel perforations repaired within 12 hours; acute cholecystitis without perforation; and ischemic, non-perforated bowel.

        Route Age Groups

      Heading
      for the treatment of complicated diverticulitis† without definitive source control
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours. Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat at least 48 hours, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total treatment duration of up to 14 days.
          Heading
          Obese Adults
          Dosage Description
          20 to 25 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day). Consider loading dose in critically ill patients. Treat at least 48 hours, followed by oral step-down therapy for a total treatment duration of up to 14 days.
    Routes
    Heading
    For the treatment of intraabdominal infections†, including peritonitis†, appendicitis†, intraabdominal abscess†, biliary tract infections† (cholecystitis†, cholangitis†), neonatal necrotizing enterocolitis†, diverticulitis†, spontaneous bacterial peritonitis†, and peritoneal dialysis-related peritonitis†

      Heading
      for the treatment of cutaneous anthrax† without aerosol exposure or signs and symptoms of meningitis
      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day) for 7 to 10 days or until clinical criteria for stability are met; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. May consider step-down to oral therapy.

          Heading
          Neonates older than 28 weeks gestation and SCr less than 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. Treat for 7 to 10 days or until clinical criteria for stability are met; may consider step-down to oral therapy.

      Heading

      Routes
        Heading
        Intravenous dosage
        Route Age Groups
          Heading
          Adults
          Dosage Description
          15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours (Usual Max: 4,500 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Treat for 7 to 10 days or until clinical criteria for stability are met; may consider step-down to oral therapy. Transition to a postexposure prophylaxis regimen to complete a 42- to 60-day total treatment course depending on vaccine status and immunocompetence.

Treat for at least 14 days; may consider step-down to oral therapy. Transition to a postexposure prophylaxis regimen to complete a 60-day total treatment course from illness onset.
          Heading
          Neonates 28 weeks gestation and younger and SCr 0.5 to 0.7 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 24 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested. Treat for at least 14 days; may consider step-down to oral therapy. Transition to a postexposure prophylaxis regimen to complete a 60-day total treatment course from illness onset.
          Heading
          Neonates 28 weeks gestation and younger and SCr 0.8 to 1 mg/dL
          Dosage Description
          20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 mg/kg/dose IV every 24 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. The dosing of vancomycin may need to be adjusted in neonatal patients on ECMO; doses of 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 18 to 24 hours have been suggested.         Heading

        20 to 35 mg/kg/dose (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV every 8 to 12 hours; adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter.         Heading
        Obese Adults
        Dosage Description

        Heading
        Children and Adolescents 12 to 17 years
        Dosage Description
        60 to 70 mg/kg/day IV divided every 6 to 8 hours (Usual Max: 3,000 mg/day; may require up to 3,600 mg/day); adjust dose based on target PK/PD parameter. Data are insufficient to recommend a loading dose in nonobese pediatric patients; however, based on adult studies, consider loading dose of 20 to 35 mg/kg (Max: 3,000 mg/dose) IV in critically ill patients. Use in combination with IV penicillin G and a macrolide as an adjunct to diphtheria antitoxin.

    Heading
    For bacterial infection prophylaxis† after penetrating trauma
    Routes
      Heading
      Oral dosage
      Route Age Groups
        Heading
        Adults
        Dosage Description
        125 mg PO once daily. May increase dose to 125 mg PO 2 or 3 times daily if patients continue to experience loose stools at lower doses. Secondary prophylaxis may be considered in patients who are not candidates for fecal microbiota transplantation, relapsed after fecal microbiota transplantation, require ongoing or frequent antibiotic courses, or are at high risk of recurrence during systemic antibiotic use.
    Heading
    For secondary C. difficile infection prophylaxis† (i.e., long-term suppressive therapy†)
    Routes
      Heading
      Intravenous dosage
      Route Age Groups
        Heading
        Adults
        Dosage Description
        20 mg/kg/dose (Max: 2,000 mg/dose) IV every 8 to 12 hours during the intrapartum period as part of alternative combination therapy. Give 1 additional dose after cesarean delivery; an additional dose is generally not needed after vaginal delivery. Other risk factors such as bacteremia or persistent postpartum fever may require additional therapy.
        Heading

        Dosage Description

      Heading
      Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage
      Route Age Groups
        Heading
        Adults
        Dosage Description
        15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L.
        Heading
        Infants, Children, and Adolescents
        Dosage Description
        10 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (age 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years.
        Heading
        Neonates
        Dosage Description
        10 to 15 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 15 to 40 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion, based on postmenstrual age and renal function, to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Study data have shown that continuous infusions are well tolerated in neonates and may also result in improved achievement of optimal target concentrations.

      Continuous Intravenous Infusion dosage†
      Route Age Groups

        Adults

        15 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by 30 to 60 mg/kg/day continuous IV infusion to achieve a steady-state concentration of 20 to 25 mg/L.
        Heading
        Infants, Children, and Adolescents
        Dosage Description
        10 to 20 mg/kg/dose IV loading dose, followed by continuous IV infusion of 30 to 60 mg/kg/day IV to achieve a steady-state concentration of 15 to 25 mcg/mL. Pediatric patients younger than 8 years have been shown to require higher doses than older patients to achieve target vancomycin serum concentrations. In a study in 240 pediatric patients (age 31 days and older) converted to a continuous infusion regimen, the mean doses required to achieve a vancomycin serum concentration of 15 to 20 mg/L were approximately 50 mg/kg/day IV in patients 8 years and younger and approximately 45 mg/kg/day IV in patients older than 8 years.
